People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. I don’t notice it as much.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble remembering things when you are not under any pressure or stress?\n\nPatient: I guess it’s mostly when there’s pressure. Like… I came in because my mind keeps going blank at bad times, with the road-closure forms or the stop-slow paddle. I’ll suddenly not remember steps I’ve done for years. When I’m not stressed, I don’t notice it as much.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious when you're not at work and there's no immediate pressure or stress?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean… sometimes I feel uneasy, but I don’t always know if it’s anxiety or just me thinking too much. When there’s no pressure, I don’t notice it as clearly.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have trouble remembering things when you are completely relaxed and there's no pressure on you?\n\nPatient: I don’t really notice it when I’m completely relaxed. It’s more… at the worst times, like with the road-closure forms or the stop-slow paddle. I don’t think I’ve noticed that, not like… losing things from before. It’s more when I’m right in front of something I should know—like the road-closure forms or the stop-slow paddle—and my mind just goes blank.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're outside of your body or watching yourself when you have these blank moments?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. It’s more like I just… freeze up or blank out, not like I’m watching myself from somewhere else.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel disconnected from yourself or your surroundings when you're not having one of these blank moments?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not that I’ve noticed. My mind just blanks, and I can’t remember steps I’ve done for years.\n\nDoctor: Do these blank moments in your mind last for a while after the stressful situation has passed, or do they go away once the pressure is off?\n\nPatient: It’s mostly right in the moment, when I’m under pressure. Like I’ll be staring at the forms or holding the paddle and… it’s just gone. I can’t remember the steps I know I know. I don’t really know how long after. It just shakes me up.\n\nDoctor: Do these memory blanks happen to you in situations outside of work as well?\n\nPatient: Mostly I notice it at work. That’s where it scares me, with the forms or the stop-slow paddle… I just suddenly can’t remember steps I’ve done for years. Outside of work, I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any situations outside of work where you've had trouble remembering something you normally know?\n\nPatient: I… I can’t really think of many outside work. It’s mostly those work things that stick out, the road-closure forms or the paddle, where I suddenly can’t remember what I’m supposed to do. Outside of that, I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or nervous in your daily life?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… maybe sometimes. I get nervous when things are happening, but I don’t really keep track of it day to day.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or nervous most of the time?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say most of the time. I mean… I get nervous, but I’m not sure it’s all the time.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble remembering things when you're relaxed and not under any pressure?\n\nPatient: Mostly it seems to happen when I’m on the spot. Like at work, I’ll look at the forms or the stop-slow paddle and my mind just… goes blank, even though I’ve done those steps for years. When I’m relaxed, I’m not sure. I don’t notice it as much.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble remembering things when you are not under any pressure or stress?\n\nPatient: I guess it’s mostly when there’s pressure. Like… I came in because my mind keeps going blank at bad times, with the road-closure forms or the stop-slow paddle. I’ll suddenly not remember steps I’ve done for years. When I’m not stressed, I don’t notice it as much.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious when you're not at work and there's no immediate pressure or stress?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean… sometimes I feel uneasy, but I don’t always know if it’s anxiety or just me thinking too much. When there’s no pressure, I don’t notice it as clearly.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have trouble remembering things when you are completely relaxed and there's no pressure on you?\n\nPatient: I don’t really notice it when I’m completely relaxed. It’s more… at the worst times, like with the road-closure forms or the stop-slow paddle. I’ll just blank on steps I’ve done for years.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble remembering things from your past that happened before you started having these blank moments at work?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I don’t think I’ve noticed that, not like… losing things from before. It’s more just the blanking when I’m trying to do something.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble remembering things that happened last week or last month?\n\nPatient: Not really like last week or last month as a whole. It’s more when I’m right in front of something I should know—like the road-closure forms or the stop-slow paddle—and my mind just goes blank.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're outside of your body or watching yourself when you have these blank moments?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. It’s more like I just… freeze up or blank out, not like I’m watching myself from somewhere else.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel disconnected from yourself or your surroundings when you're not having one of these blank moments?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not that I’ve noticed. I mean, I can feel kind of off sometimes, but not really disconnected like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're in a daze or detached from what's happening around you?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I get kind of foggy sometimes, but I wouldn’t say detached. I’m still aware of what’s going on around me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're not yourself or like things aren't real when you're not having one of these blank moments?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I haven’t really felt like things aren’t real. Maybe just tired or foggy sometimes, but not that.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any traumatic events in your life that might be connected to when these blank moments started happening?\n\nPatient: I… I’m not sure. I don’t really have anything like that I can point to. I haven’t connected it to some specific event.\n\nDoctor: Did your blank moments start happening after something significantly stressful or upsetting occurred in your life?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess so. It started after my closest friend died. It’s been… about a year and eight months now.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
